He prepares the Turkish Farmakope. He takes precautions for drugs to be found in the market, makes pharmacoeconomic assessments to determine drug prices. It is the basis of the legal protection of our country in 1871, in matters of the Order of Alamet-iFirika and in terms of the 1879 Law on Dissolution, brands and patents. With these regulations, Turkey is among the countries that ensure the protection of test property rights. Again, in 1925, we sided with the Paris Convention on Test property law, which provided the basis for the establishment of an international union for the preservation of testing property in the early years of our republic. In 1965, the Marka Law No. 551 took effect, and in 1976, participation in the World Fictional Property Association Agreement was another important step in protecting our country's testing property rights, and the Turkish Patent Institute, where we talked about its budget, was established and launched in 1994. The Turkish Patent Institute, which continues its activities as a special budgetary organisation under the Ministry of Science, Industry and Technology, is at the centre of the task and responsibilities-testing property system. It is an undeniable fact that the TPE (TPE) has achieved very important achievements in the framework of the 2012-2016 Strategy Plan on testing property rights, running fast and quality property rights, disseminating testing property culture to all parts of the community, and making testing property into economic benefits, being driving national and international platforms in the field of testing property, and finally systematically strengthening institutional capacity. tbmm-2014-12-18sit05spe0215par0385	Mr. President, we held a meeting last week with the executives of small industrial sites, especially in Istanbul, the Two-seat Organised Industrial District, the Dudullı Organised Industrial Region, and I'm here to share an assessment with you in the Grand National Assembly of Turkey. SMEs, as in the world, are one of the dynamic and riveting elements of the Turkish economy, and are very important for our country's socioeconomic development. The confusion over the description of the KOBI is also an obstacle to a healthy inventory study. In the globalisation process, conservation is disappearing, freeing foreign capital flow, easier to interact with each other through foreign trade channels and a high competitive environment. In the process, there is a market structure in which the government's role and support in the economy has been reduced and privatisation is more serious. From this point of view, the impact of the globalization process on the country's economies and on companies that are actors of these economies is inevitable. The quotas made by countries to protect themselves are a sign of a tendency or a lack of globalization in the face of customs walls. For this reason, regionalisation and regional integration, a conflicting trend with globalisation. While there are different views on whether regionalisation is a blocker of globalization or a facilitator, it is the trend of action with regionalisation and the focus of economic integration that provides a good basis for globalisation. In fact, in recent years, especially regional formations in Europe and later developments have indicated this. In Turkey, SMEs are seen to be largely interested in fason production, creating their own brands and having difficulty marketing their own products. The lack of country image and brands weakens the competitiveness of these enterprises greatly. From this point of view, the role of Turkey in the globalization process is largely the country's cheap, fason-producing position. Companies lose their competitive advantage over these developments as long as they can't create their own brand and don't care about quality production. On the other hand, it is important to ignore that the decision being made by the minors and their flexibility in the practice provide them with ease in adapting to new developments. The point is that financial and other necessary supports can be provided in time, determined how small or medium-scale businesses should follow a strategy that faces a competitive market, but is weak in competitiveness. There are basic problems with the production costs of the COBI. The main goal of all businesses is to give quality to their customers cheaply. In order to do that, they need to minimize production costs. AN application to reduce the costs of the COBI is an exchange or barter activity. With the establishment of exchange organisations, the COBIs are able to exchange goods and services with each other without trading money -- within the organization. In this way, businesses are able to use their drop capacity and use discounts in raw materials supply. Memberships can improve market share in foreign markets by using marketing information systems. In addition, companies are able to work together to benefit from scale economies, such as large businesses, and thus reduce their costs. COSGEB and COBI are required to support software programmes in order to reduce production costs and allow them to use technology. The concept of modern marketing acknowledges that it all starts with the market. For this reason, the success of the KOBI is important to focus on the market. My dear fellow lawmakers, our economy has continued to grow every year since 2002, except for the year of the crisis. The last 12 years of growth performance have been significantly higher than the average of both countries in the European Union and the OECD countries. At the coming point, we have completed substantially structural reforms, established public financial balances, controlled budget deficits, and are safely on our way. In addition, we have disciplined the banking sector, the financial sector and our entire economy have actually passed many tests, including the 2008 global crisis. The main reason why we left the global market crisis in 2008-09 with minimal damage is the industrial sector in Turkey. Look, if our economy wasn't based on production, in 2008, this country would have had a much bigger crisis than the 2001 crisis. If it weren't for the power of the manufacturing industry in our economy, the 2008 crisis would have turned into a nightmare for Turkey as it did for many of our neighbors. When the eleven-year period between 2003-2013 was examined, the industrial sector, particularly the manufacturing industry, has grown faster than the country's growth. Look, especially the manufacturing industry has led to growth. Over the past 11 years, Turkey has averaged, including the crisis, 5 per cent growth, while our manufacturing industry has grown 5.8%. The industry is ruined, it has become miserable, it has become small. There is no consistentness in its words. By the way, the growth of the country, especially this year under our expectations, is 2.8, but the growth of the manufacturing industry is 3.5 again. So the manufacturing industry is actually an important factor in our total growth. The first is to provide a price advantage for domestic property in public purchases. If this purchase is a technological product, we're not leaving it at the mercy of public authority, even if it's 15 percent expensive, it's going to buy domestic product.
